=== mpv prescalers: the filter's runner against the harness's ===
  [ok  ] WIC factory

The prescalers the filter embeds, run by its own code (Source/Upscale/MpvShader.cpp) and by the
harness (mpvhook.inl), on the luma of each reference reduced to half size.

psnr: the two pictures against each other, 99 when they are identical; max: the largest difference
in a plane whose values run from 0 to 1. ms: the filter's runner, GPU time of one picture.

========================================================================
[STEP] upscalers on film
[CMD ] dlssnr_harness.exe --tupscale --nonr --nomodels
========================================================================
CDlssNR harness -- the filter's own code, outside the player
===========================================================

=== D3D11 device ===
  [ok  ] D3D11CreateDevice

=== Upscaling suite: what each method rebuilds from a reduced picture ===
  [ok  ] WIC factory
  [ok  ] resize shaders compiled
  mpv shader ArtCNN_C4F16, 8 passes
  mpv shader ArtCNN_C4F16_DS, 8 passes
  mpv shader ArtCNN_C4F32, 8 passes
  mpv shader FSRCNNX_x2_16-0-4-1, 26 passes
  mpv shader FSRCNNX_x2_8-0-4-1, 14 passes
  mpv shader nnedi3-nns32-win8x4, 4 passes, output shifted by -0.50, -0.50 pixels (taken back with Catmull-Rom,
    as the player's own resize would)
  mpv shader ravu-lite-ar-r4, 2 passes
  mpv shader ravu-zoom-ar-r3, 1 passes
  [ok  ] timestamp queries

Upscaling on real pictures. Each reference is reduced by an integer factor, degraded the way a
source is, then brought back to the reference size by each method and compared to the reference.

psnr and ssim: luma, against the reference; psnr-d: psnr on the reference's most detailed quarter,
where the eye sees the difference. sharp: gradient on the reference's edges, output over
reference, so 1.00 is the reference's own sharpness. halo: overshoot past the reference's local
range on those edges, lower is better. grain: fine detail left in flat areas, output over reference.
block: on the compressed rows only, the steps sitting on the compression grid against the steps
elsewhere, so 1.00 means the blocks are gone. ms: GPU time of the resize itself.
mpv shaders (translated by mpv_shaders.py) run on the reduced picture's luma, as mpv runs them;
a doubler that falls short of the size is finished by the renderer's Catmull-Rom, timed with it.

=== Chroma suite: what each upsampler rebuilds from 4:2:0 ===
  [ok  ] WIC factory
  mpv shader KrigBilateral, 3 passes
  mpv shader CfL_Prediction, 4 passes
  mpv shader CfL_Prediction_Lite, 2 passes
  mpv luma shader FSRCNNX_x2_8-0-4-1 on each chroma plane, 14 passes
  mpv luma shader FSRCNNX_x2_16-0-4-1 on each chroma plane, 26 passes
  mpv luma shader ravu-zoom-ar-r3 on each chroma plane, 1 passes
  mpv luma shader ravu-lite-ar-r4 on each chroma plane, 2 passes
  mpv luma shader ArtCNN_C4F16 on each chroma plane, 8 passes
  mpv luma shader ArtCNN_C4F16_DS on each chroma plane, 8 passes
  mpv luma shader nnedi3-nns32-win8x4 on each chroma plane, 4 passes

Chroma upsampling from 4:2:0. Each reference is turned into Y'CbCr (BT.709, full range), its chroma
averaged over 2x2 blocks with centred siting, brought back to full size by each method, and the picture
turned back into RGB.

psnr-c: Cb and Cr against the reference's; psnr-e: the same where the luma has edges, where bleeding
shows; ssim-c: chroma SSIM; psnr-rgb: the whole picture. ms: GPU time of the shader passes.
The luma doublers (FSRCNNX, RAVU) see Cb, then Cr, each alone as the plane they hook; their time
covers both planes.

========================================================================
[STEP] chroma through the filter, 10-bit
========================================================================
picture: 800x450, reduced by 2 from the file
Film 800x450 at 23.976 fps in a 800x450 window, 3 s per configuration after 5 s to settle.
BT.709, limited range, chroma sited left, shown at 1:1 with the dither off.
psnr-c: Cb and Cr of the displayed picture against the 4:4:4 one; psnr-e: the
same where the luma has edges, where bleeding shows; offset: the constant
shift of Cb and Cr, in 8-bit levels.
